Graph cannot drop the entry, not even after the clone's want goes Obsolete. A seat that graph never re-reads after a Herdr restart, for example because threads is unreachable, gets no row, although design \u00a74.5 lists `never attempted because threads was unreachable` as a case the row covers."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":11,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] src/daemon/clone_status.rs:75","text":"[suggested Should-fix] src/daemon/clone_status.rs:75 \u2014 The `restart_held` status row and doctor finding do not check that the clone's recorded pane still exists. A seated clone whose pane vanished in the restart gets a `restart_held` row (alongside `resume_blocked`) whose remedy tells the operator to rebind onto a pane that no longer exists, or onto the literal pane \"unknown\" when the binding was cleared."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":12,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] CHANGELOG.md:22","text":"[suggested Should-fix] CHANGELOG.md:22 \u2014 The CHANGELOG states unconditionally that an undo-restored seat whose agent exits now gets the ordinary exited-agent relaunch, but the fix only skips the launch record for keyless clones; a keyed undo-restored clone still records `launched:` and so remains excluded from start_needed."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":13,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] src/threads/effects.rs:1866","text":"[suggested Should-fix] src/threads/effects.rs:1866 \u2014 The resurrect paths write two stores without a shared transaction: the refusal path does `meta_set(resurrect_refused:<clone>)` in the journal and then a separate tree write via `record_refusal` whose error is discarded, and the success/ack paths admit the tree transition and then `let _ = meta_delete(...)`. A crash or error between the two leaves the journal meta and the clone record disagreeing."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":14,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] docs/superpowers/runs/2026-11-26-resurrect-seat-frictionless-restart/profile-9b510293-b854-4149-b916-64e7b38c421d.json:10","text":"[suggested Should-fix] docs/superpowers/runs/2026-11-26-resurrect-seat-frictionless-restart/profile-9b510293-b854-4149-b916-64e7b38c421d.json:10 \u2014 The diff commits a run profile to a public GitHub repository that records about 164 absolute local paths, the OS username and the agent session id, including paths to the full session transcripts under the user's home directory."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":15,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/support/threads_bin.rs:98","text":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/support/threads_bin.rs:98 \u2014 The five new tier-5 tests in threads_real depend on an HG_TEST_THREADS_SUPPORT_BINARY env input that the Herdr release matrix runner never supplies, so under that runner every one of them panics instead of running."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":16,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/e2e_private_herdr.rs:3768","text":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/e2e_private_herdr.rs:3768 \u2014 The four new e2e_private_herdr tests are not in the release matrix's exact-match e2e inventory, which matched every base #[test] before this diff, so the matrix's e2e suite now fails classification for every Herdr version."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":17,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/threads_real.rs:841","text":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/threads_real.rs:841 \u2014 Tier5::new builds graph's production threads ports inside the test process, and Endpoint::from_env there reads the test process's ambient HERDR_GRAPH_THREADS_SOCKET/HERDR_GRAPH_THREADS_INSTANCE, which take precedence over the private env_state_dir; nothing checks the discovered socket against the private root."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":18,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/e2e_private_herdr.rs:3828","text":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/e2e_private_herdr.rs:3828 \u2014 late_rebind_binds_the_fresh_agent_at_its_next_prompt accepts both rebind orders and only proves the late-path escalation inside `if late`, so it can pass without exercising the behavior it is named for."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":19,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/adopt_private_herdr.rs:1077","text":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/adopt_private_herdr.rs:1077 \u2014 close_restart_then_undo_resurrects_the_seat never waits for graph to observe the Herdr restart: its \"graph to answer after the restart\" wait is met by the graph daemon, which never went down, and `settle()` (1.5 s with no commits) can return inside graph's 5 s resubscribe backoff. The \"still closed after the restart\" assertions and the undo can therefore run against the pre-restart incarnation."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":20,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/integration_sweep.rs:3316","text":"[suggested Should-fix] tests/integration_sweep.rs:3316 \u2014 In the after-7d window of keyless_close_then_undo, the `call_count(\"resurrect_seat\") == 0` check is sampled at the first moment the clone shows a reopened pane, before the pass that would issue a ResurrectSeat, so the test passes even if graph resurrects on a stale reversible fact."},{"section":"Not verified (beyond panel cap)","position":21,"key":"[suggested Should-fix] docs/superpowers/runs/2026-11-26-resurrect-seat-frictionless-restart/run.md:53","text":"[suggested Should-fix] docs/superpowers/runs/2026-11-26-resurrect-seat-frictionless-restart/run.md:53 \u2014 The PR head carries its own final review whose recorded verdict is 'not ready' with unresolved must-fix items, so the branch is being presented for review in a state its own process says is not landable."},{"section":"Beyond remainder cap (count only)","position":1,"key":"44 candidates dropped by the remainder cap","text":"44 candidates dropped by the remainder cap \u2014 raise config.remainderCap and re-run to see them"},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":1,"key":"src/threads/effects.rs:1919","text":"src/threads/effects.rs:1919 \u2014 Rebind NotFound is recorded as a definitive refusal for the whole incarnation, contradicting design 4.2 and leaving the seat unbound if threads answers NotFound transiently before its restore hold is in place.\n  reason: 3/3 REJECT. The threads U2 design (herdr-threads docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-26-service-restore-held-seat-design.md, D2/D3/D5) answers the pre-hold window with ServiceBusy (retryable), not NotFound. NotFound is reserved for definitive cases, so recording `rebind_refused` once per incarnation matches design 4.2. The already-placed case returns SeatAlreadyResolved, which the code maps to Done. Design 4.2's \"NotFound ... already placed\" wording is stale prose only."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":2,"key":"src/threads/effects.rs:1907","text":"src/threads/effects.rs:1907 \u2014 The restart rebind uses one threads op key per incarnation (`rebind:<clone>:<changed_at>`), so if the seat turns Unresolved a second time in the same incarnation after an earlier rebind succeeded, the retry gets threads' stored success replayed under that key and graph records Done without the seat being placed.\n  reason: 2 REJECT, 1 CONFIRM at Nit. Design section 4 states the choice: \"one op key per incarnation, so a want is re-planned, not duplicated\". The scenario needs a seat to be rebound and then read Unresolved again inside the same Herdr incarnation; no mechanism for that was shown, and a restart changes the key. The ground seat's CONFIRM cites herdr-threads docs/design/graph-system-identity/design.md:69 for the replay behaviour, which the majority does not dispute; it concedes the same reachability limit and the 4.5 `restart_held` backstop. The majority evidence answers the dissent, so the default stands. Residual: a key-design nit (add a discriminator such as the fact `read_at`)."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":3,"key":"src/threads/effects.rs:1798; src/threads/effects.rs:1802","text":"src/threads/effects.rs:1798; src/threads/effects.rs:1802 \u2014 A definitively refused resurrect is re-asked only when replace or the pane changes, not when the seat fact changes or after any time-out, so the undo's restore launch stays blocked indefinitely after the refusal's cause clears.\n  reason: 3/3 REJECT. Design section 3 says a definitive refusal is recorded once and the want is re-planned when its inputs change. The want is keyed by (seat, pane, epoch) and the executor returns Obsolete when `resurrectable()` no longer matches, so a changed fact is covered; `replace` and pane are compared directly. The design states no time-based retry and names the operator remedy via `clones.resurrect_held` after the 90 s grace. The transient-local-error part of this claim is the confirmed finding at src/ports/threads.rs:179."},{"section":"Rejected (with reason)","position":4,"key":"src/threads/adapter.rs:62; src/threads/adapter.rs:792; src/threads/adapter.rs:801","text":"src/threads/adapter.rs:62; src/threads/adapter.rs:792; src/threads/adapter.rs:801 \u2014 Graph registers only service_session_v4 with no v3 or Notify fallback, so against an older threads daemon every threads operation (including summarizer delivery) hard-fails, and the resulting terminal Failed effects are not re-attempted after the daemon is upgraded.\n  reason: 3/3 REJECT. Design line 63: \"An older daemon refuses the v4 registration, and graph's existing registration-failure path reports it (no silent fallback to v3).\" CHANGELOG Migration says \"Upgrade herdr-threads first\" and doctor names the upgrade step. Removing the Notify degrade follows from that stated choice. Terminal Failed rows not being re-attempted is the same Rejected-to-Failed behaviour base had below the v3 floor."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":1,"key":"src/undo/preview.rs:486","text":"src/undo/preview.rs:486 \u2014 The undo-preview window note returns nothing for any fact that reads `keyed: true`. Because `keyed` defaults to true for legacy facts, a keyless clone whose retire-time fact was written before this change and reads `Retired{reversible:false}` gets no warning that undo gives the pane a fresh seat and loses memberships graph does not own.\n  spot check: CONFIRM at Nit. `seat_window_note` matches `Retired { keyed: true, .. } => return None` before the `reversible: false` arm, and legacy facts deserialize as keyed:true. Same root cause as the confirmed finding at src/model/clone.rs:100."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":2,"key":"tests/e2e_private_herdr.rs:3784","text":"tests/e2e_private_herdr.rs:3784 \u2014 The late-rebind test's fixed 95 s sleep leaves about 5 s of margin over the 90 s grace, which graph's own incarnation record can lag by the resubscribe backoff, so the test may quietly take the non-late order.\n  spot check: CONFIRM at Nit. The grace is measured from graph's journalled `incarnation:changed_at`, and src/observe/step.rs:1056 has a 5 s resubscribe sleep. The final assertions are order-independent, so a non-late run still verifies the end state."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":3,"key":"src/reconcile/tests.rs:3919","text":"src/reconcile/tests.rs:3919 \u2014 restart_arm_needs_an_earlier_agent now asserts the opposite of its name: a clone with only a `launched:` meta and no session is relaunched. The original invariant, that the resume rule does not launch a seated clone that never had an agent, now has only shell-harness or keyless coverage.\n  spot check: REJECT. The change is intended (spec 4.3: a `launched:` meta counts as had-agent). The never-launched path is still covered by the `Had::Nothing` arm in src/reconcile/tests.rs (~5497-5534). What remains is a stale test name."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":4,"key":"src/reconcile/herdr_exec.rs:38","text":"src/reconcile/herdr_exec.rs:38 \u2014 The keyless fresh-start gate and the restart_covered change alter agent launch behavior after every Herdr restart with no flag: fresh keyless starts are now delayed up to the 90 s grace, and clones with only a `launched:` meta (never observed with an agent) are now relaunched by the restart rule.\n  spot check: REJECT. Both behaviours are stated design: section 4 \"Gate\" and \"Accepted behaviour\" bound the wait at the 90 s grace, and section 4.3 states the `launched:` rule."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":5,"key":"CHANGELOG.md:29","text":"CHANGELOG.md:29 \u2014 During the documented skew window (threads 3.6.0 daemon, old graph still running), the old graph sees keyless pane closes as reversible retirements and its unretire want issues UnretireSeat for keyless seats on undo, a combination the diff does not test or document.\n  spot check: REJECT. Design line 37 already documents this degradation, and the behaviour lives in the base graph binary, not in this diff. The claim is external and the single spot seat did not mark it UNVERIFIED."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":6,"key":"src/threads/effects.rs:1841","text":"src/threads/effects.rs:1841 \u2014 A resurrect refused as NotFound returns Deferred and records no meta, so every reconcile step until the next seat read changes the fact re-issues a full durable ResurrectSeat mutation: a ResolveSeat RPC, the intent-journal write, the daemon call and forget_completed.\n  spot check: REJECT. Design (resurrect outcomes, ~line 80): \"NotFound: deferred. The next fact read picks the row\". Writing no meta for NotFound is deliberate."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":7,"key":"src/daemon/clone_status.rs:75; src/daemon/clone_status.rs:83","text":"src/daemon/clone_status.rs:75; src/daemon/clone_status.rs:83 \u2014 The restart_held row uses the fixed SEAT_LOST_AFTER (90 s) while the gate it describes uses the configurable relaunch_grace, and the tests hardcode 89/91 s for both so drift goes undetected.\n  spot check: REJECT. `relaunch_grace` has no config-file or CLI source; production uses 90 s for both. The design says \"no new grace constant is added\"."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":8,"key":"src/daemon/clone_status.rs:102","text":"src/daemon/clone_status.rs:102 \u2014 The restart_held and resurrect_held remedies can contain the literal `--pane unknown`, which produces a pasteable operator command that cannot work.\n  spot check: REJECT. Matches the base convention (`key_conflicts` already emits pane \"unknown\"), and the row carries `\"pane\":\"unknown\"` plus a reason."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":9,"key":"src/daemon/clone_status.rs:66","text":"src/daemon/clone_status.rs:66 \u2014 The doctor and status remedy strings put the seat id and pane id into a shell command line without quoting, and an operator is meant to copy and run that line.\n  spot check: REJECT. Seat ids are threads-minted and pane ids Herdr-generated; no user-chosen string reaches these fields, and graph does not execute the printed remedy."},{"section":"Unverified nits (spot-checked)","position":10,"key":"src/threads/adapter.rs:1045","text":"src/threads/adapter.rs:1045 \u2014 When the daemon is older than 3.6.0, every resurrect and rebind is classified as the refusal `Unsupported`. The resulting resurrect_held and restart_held rows tell the operator to run a manual `herdr-threads human seat rebind` rather than to upgrade the daemon; the upgrade hint appears only in the separate `threads` doctor check, and only through a substring match.\n  spot check: REJECT. The design lists Unsupported among definitive refusals surfaced by `resurrect_held`; src/daemon/doctor.rs:112 emits the upgrade finding and service_tests.rs:669-706 tests its wording."},{"section":"Escalations (need human)","position":1,"key":"src/threads/effects.rs:552","text":"src/threads/effects.rs:552 \u2014 The restart rebind is only wanted when the clone's last recorded `runtime.bound` pane id is present in the post-restart snapshot. On a cold restart that renumbers pane ids, a held seated clone gets no rebind and falls through to `restart_held`/operator action. That would fail B2 for that restart shape.\n  reason: UNVERIFIED external (reproduce seat UNVERIFIED; refute and ground REJECT). Open fact: whether a real Herdr restart ever renumbers pane ids. In-repo evidence says no: docs/herdr-spikes.md Spike 3 records \"pane ids reused: yes (w1:p1..p4)\" and the tier-3 tests assert `starts[1].0 == pane`. The reproduce seat notes the spike words it as luck, not a guarantee, and found no authoritative Herdr source. Cheapest check: read the Herdr source for pane id assignment on session restore, or cold-restart a private rig several times after closing a pane and compare ids."},{"section":"Escalations (need human)","position":2,"key":"src/threads/effects.rs:636","text":"src/threads/effects.rs:636 \u2014 A keyless pending Restore whose seat reads `Unresolved` on a verified pane is acknowledged by the succession path, with no resurrect and no rebind. That acknowledgement is written as `AcknowledgeResurrected`, which resets ordinary invitations even though the seat was never brought back.\n  reason: UNVERIFIED external (ground seat UNVERIFIED; reproduce and refute REJECT). Pre-existing on base: 60e287aa src/threads/effects.rs:553 (the `F::Unresolved` acknowledge arm is unchanged), so FYI at most. Open fact: whether threads 3.6.0 can report a just-closed keyless seat as Unresolved before Retired. The ground seat read store/seats/observation.rs and could not rule out an unresolved-then-retired sequence. Cheapest check: on a private Herdr, close an adopted keyless tab, undo at once, and read the seat fact stream for an Unresolved sample before `Retired{reversible}`."}]}

- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-2/cluster-1","artifact":"reviews/inv-2/final-review-inv-2.cluster-1.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"ready","occurrences":[{"section":"Untested scope","position":1,"key":"No test runs graph against a real 3.5.x daemon; the floor is covered at unit level (`daemon_floor_refuses_below_3_6_0_by_name` and the published-descriptor test in src/threads/service_tests.rs) and by the tier-5 retryable-refusal check (hg-lnw.29), not by a live 3.5.x process.","text":"No test runs graph against a real 3.5.x daemon; the floor is covered at unit level (`daemon_floor_refuses_below_3_6_0_by_name` and the published-descriptor test in src/threads/service_tests.rs) and by the tier-5 retryable-refusal check (hg-lnw.29), not by a live 3.5.x process."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":2,"key":"The remedy string printed by status, doctor and the docs is not parsed and run end to end against real threads; the tier-5 operator rebind test builds its own argv (carried from inv-1).","text":"The remedy string printed by status, doctor and the docs is not parsed and run end to end against real threads; the tier-5 operator rebind test builds its own argv (carried from inv-1)."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":1,"key":"`daemon_floor` strips pre-release suffixes, so a `3.6.0-rc.N` daemon passes the floor although semver orders it below 3.6.0; negligible for released daemons.","text":"`daemon_floor` strips pre-release suffixes, so a `3.6.0-rc.N` daemon passes the floor although semver orders it below 3.6.0; negligible for released daemons."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":2,"key":"An empty `/tmp/.git` directory, not created by this epic, breaks `stop-run-processes` temp-root checks; a human should remove it (`rmdir /tmp/.git`) (carried from inv-1).","text":"An empty `/tmp/.git` directory, not created by this epic, breaks `stop-run-processes` temp-root checks; a human should remove it (`rmdir /tmp/.git`) (carried from inv-1)."}]}

- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-2/cluster-26","artifact":"reviews/inv-2/final-review-inv-2.cluster-26.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"ready","occurrences":[{"section":"Concerns","position":1,"key":"A type error inside a `CloneRecord` (for example a malformed id or timestamp in a clone TOML) now comes back through `D::Error::custom` from the `Value` pass. It loses the TOML line/column span the derived impl reported. Syntax errors still carry spans. This is a diagnostic regression for hand-edited or corrupted records, which the implementer also noted."},{"section":"Concerns","position":2,"key":"A `bookkeeping.seat_fact` change request queued by a pre-upgrade build and applied after the upgrade parses its standalone `SeatFact` with the `keyed: true` default, even for a keyless clone. The effect is self-healing and narrow.","text":"A `bookkeeping.seat_fact` change request queued by a pre-upgrade build and applied after the upgrade parses its standalone `SeatFact` with the `keyed: true` default, even for a keyless clone. The next SeatObservation pass re-reads the fact from threads and overwrites it, so at most one plan can route a keyless reversible seat to unretire. Threads refuses that and the want is re-planned. The effect is self-healing and narrow."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":1,"key":"No test reads a legacy clone record whose seat fact lacks both `keyed` and `retired_at` through the effects planner end to end, to confirm a pre-upgrade keyless reversible fact is routed to resurrect and not unretire. This is a diagnostic-only regression; a follow-up could deserialize through `toml::Value`, or patch the table before the typed pass, to keep spans.","text":"TOML type errors inside `CloneRecord` lose their line/column span because they go through `serde_json::Value`. This is a diagnostic-only regression; a follow-up could deserialize through `toml::Value`, or patch the table before the typed pass, to keep spans."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":2,"key":"A pre-upgrade queued `bookkeeping.seat_fact` request on a keyless clone reads `keyed: true` until the next observation pass overwrites it. This is self-healing within one pass.","text":"A pre-upgrade queued `bookkeeping.seat_fact` request on a keyless clone reads `keyed: true` until the next observation pass overwrites it. - finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-2/cluster-29","artifact":"reviews/inv-2/final-review-inv-2.cluster-29.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"ready","occurrences":[{"section":"Concerns","position":1,"key":"`ThreadsError::Rejected`'s doc comment says graph-local failures are never `Rejected` and name \"intent journal\" among the `graph-local: `-prefixed `Disconnected` errors. The adapter's intent-journal and completion-repair errors are `Disconnected(\"intent journal: ...\")` and `Disconnected(\"completion record needs repair\")`, with no prefix (`src/threads/adapter.rs:397-402`). `mutate_raw` also returns a graph-local `Rejected(\"mutate: operation has no key\")` (`src/threads/adapter.rs:422`). The behaviour is correct: the first two are retryable. The third cannot happen for `ResurrectSeat`, which always carries a key. Only the comment overstates.","text":"`ThreadsError::Rejected`'s doc comment says graph-local failures are never `Rejected` and name \"intent journal\" among the `graph-local: `-prefixed `Disconnected` errors. The adapter's intent-journal and completion-repair errors are `Disconnected(\"intent journal: ...\")` and `Disconnected(\"completion record needs repair\")`, with no prefix (`src/threads/adapter.rs:397-402`). `mutate_raw` also returns a graph-local `Rejected(\"mutate: operation has no key\")` (`src/threads/adapter.rs:422`). The behaviour is correct: the first two are retryable. The third cannot happen for `ResurrectSeat`, which always carries a key. Only the comment overstates."},{"section":"Concerns","position":2,"key":"`SeatRestoreOutcome::Refused { retryable }` is a public, serialized field. Its agreement with `reason` is held only by convention plus the `refused` constructor. The executors route on the flag, not on `reason.retryable()`, so a hand-built or deserialized inconsistent value would misroute. No production path does either today.","text":"`SeatRestoreOutcome::Refused { retryable }` is a public, serialized field. Its agreement with `reason` is held only by convention plus the `refused` constructor. The executors route on the flag, not on `reason.retryable()`, so a hand-built or deserialized inconsistent value would misroute. No production path does either today."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":1,"key":"No unit test asserts `SeatRestoreRefusal::retryable()` for every variant directly. No test asserts that the production `LazyThreads` overrides both. - finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-3/cluster-23","artifact":"reviews/inv-3/final-review-inv-3.cluster-23.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"ready","occurrences":[{"section":"Findings","position":1,"key":"`restart_held` does not check that the bound pane still exists. A clone whose pane vanished across the restart gets a `resume_blocked` row (\"retire the clone\") and, if its fact reads `Unresolved` after the grace, also a `restart_held` row whose remedy rebinds onto that missing pane. Spec §4 keeps \"pane missing -> resume_blocked unchanged\"; the second, contradictory doctor WARN is operator noise, not a functional defect (the planner does not emit the rebind there).","text":"`restart_held` does not check that the bound pane still exists. A clone whose pane vanished across the restart gets a `resume_blocked` row (\"retire the clone\") and, if its fact reads `Unresolved` after the grace, also a `restart_held` row whose remedy rebinds onto that missing pane. Spec §4 keeps \"pane missing -> resume_blocked unchanged\"; the second, contradictory doctor WARN is operator noise, not a functional defect (the planner does not emit the rebind there)."},{"section":"Findings","position":2,"key":"The doctor wiring of `restart_held_check` / `resurrect_held_check` into `doctor()` has no doctor-level test; the check functions and the status rows are unit-tested, and the status rows are asserted in tier-3 and sweep tests.","text":"The doctor wiring of `restart_held_check` / `resurrect_held_check` into `doctor()` has no doctor-level test; the check functions and the status rows are unit-tested, and the status rows are asserted in tier-3 and sweep tests."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":1,"key":"`doctor()` including the `restart held` and `resurrect held` findings in a full report (only the per-check functions are unit-tested).","text":"`doctor()` including the `restart held` and `resurrect held` findings in a full report (only the per-check functions are unit-tested)."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":2,"key":"A permanent graph-local misconfiguration on non-restore effects now retrying as `Transient` indefinitely has no test pinning the intended behaviour.","text":"A permanent graph-local misconfiguration on non-restore effects now retrying as `Transient` indefinitely has no test pinning the intended behaviour."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":1,"key":"Suppress the `restart_held` row (or its rebind remedy) for a clone that also has a `resume_blocked` row, so doctor does not print two contradictory remedies for a missing pane.","text":"Suppress the `restart_held` row (or its rebind remedy) for a clone that also has a `resume_blocked` row, so doctor does not print two contradictory remedies for a missing pane."}]}

- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-3/cluster-28","artifact":"reviews/inv-3/final-review-inv-3.cluster-28.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"ready","occurrences":[{"section":"Concerns","position":1,"key":"`SeatRestoreOutcome::Refused { reason, retryable }` has public fields and derives `Deserialize`, so the \"only constructor\" invariant (`retryable == reason.retryable()`) is a convention rather than something the type enforces. No producer at head violates it (all go through `refused()`), and the value is not persisted. Low risk.","text":"`SeatRestoreOutcome::Refused { reason, retryable }` has public fields and derives `Deserialize`, so the \"only constructor\" invariant (`retryable == reason.retryable()`) is a convention rather than something the type enforces. No producer at head violates it (all go through `refused()`), and the value is not persisted. Low risk."},{"section":"Concerns","position":2,"key":"`from_error` maps every `ThreadsError::Rejected` to `InvalidRequest`. That is right for the documented contract, but a future caller handing it the `level_refused` error (itself a `Rejected`) would record `invalid_request` instead of `unsupported` in `resurrect_refused`/`rebind_refused` meta and doctor text. Today the only path that can see that error pre-filters it in `restore_outcome`. Both reasons are definitive, so behaviour is the same; only the operator-facing reason label would be wrong.","text":"`from_error` maps every `ThreadsError::Rejected` to `InvalidRequest`. That is right for the documented contract, but a future caller handing it the `level_refused` error (itself a `Rejected`) would record `invalid_request` instead of `unsupported` in `resurrect_refused`/`rebind_refused` meta and doctor text. Today the only path that can see that error pre-filters it in `restore_outcome`. Both reasons are definitive, so behaviour is the same; only the operator-facing reason label would be wrong."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":1,"key":"No cargo run in this review (the background sweep owns the integration worktree); port-type behaviour relies on src/threads/tests.rs and src/threads/service_tests.rs retryable/from_error tests, not re-run here.","text":"No cargo run in this review (the background sweep owns the integration worktree); port-type behaviour relies on src/threads/tests.rs and src/threads/service_tests.rs retryable/from_error tests, not re-run here."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":2,"key":"That threads keeps a keyed seat's continuity_key row while its retirement is reversible (so `keyed` reads true and undo picks UnretireSeat rather than ResurrectSeat) is a mapping/real-threads property outside this cluster; seam or tier-5 reviewer to confirm.","text":"That threads keeps a keyed seat's continuity_key row while its retirement is reversible (so `keyed` reads true and undo picks UnretireSeat rather than ResurrectSeat) is a mapping/real-threads property outside this cluster; seam or tier-5 reviewer to confirm."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":1,"key":"Make `SeatRestoreOutcome::Refused` fields private or drop `Deserialize` so the retryable invariant is type-enforced.","text":"Make `SeatRestoreOutcome::Refused` fields private or drop `Deserialize` so the retryable invariant is type-enforced."},{"section":"Deferred OK","position":2,"key":"Have `from_error` (or its callers) route the `level_refused` error to `Unsupported`, so the recorded reason stays right if a new caller passes it through.","text":"Have `from_error` (or its callers) route the `level_refused` error to `Unsupported`, so the recorded reason stays right if a new caller passes it through."}]}
- finding-source · {"version":1,"source":"inv-3/cluster-29","artifact":"reviews/inv-3/final-review-inv-3.cluster-29.md","kind":"final-review","verdict":"ready","occurrences":[{"section":"Concerns","position":1,"key":"The spec section 4 row \"adopted (keyless), no session, or resume fails\" promises a fresh start after a failed resume. No reconcile path does that. `RelaunchOccupant` sets `resumed:<clone>` (one relaunch per incarnation) and records `launched:`, so after a `--resume` that exits at once, neither `plan_resume` nor `start_needed` starts the agent again. The seat is still placed by the rebind, so the threads hold the user objected to (B2) is gone. Only the agent stays down until someone starts it. Keyed clones behave the same way, and did at base. This is a spec-table gap, not a B2 regression, but nothing tests it or documents it.","text":"The spec section 4 row \"adopted (keyless), no session, or resume fails\" promises a fresh start after a failed resume. No reconcile path does that. `RelaunchOccupant` sets `resumed:<clone>` (one relaunch per incarnation) and records `launched:`, so after a `--resume` that exits at once, neither `plan_resume` nor `start_needed` starts the agent again. The seat is still placed by the rebind, so the threads hold the user objected to (B2) is gone. Only the agent stays down until someone starts it. Keyed clones behave the same way, and did at base. This is a spec-table gap, not a B2 regression, but nothing tests it or documents it."},{"section":"Concerns","position":2,"key":"The `RelaunchOccupant` and post-restart ordinary start paths still record `launched:` for keyless clones. After a restart relaunch, an adopted clone whose agent later exits with the pane open is no longer relaunched by `start_needed`, the same effect hg-lnw.3 removed for the restore path. This is pre-existing at base, and spec 4.4 covers only the undo-restore case.","text":"The `RelaunchOccupant` and post-restart ordinary start paths still record `launched:` for keyless clones. After a restart relaunch, an adopted clone whose agent later exits with the pane open is no longer relaunched by `start_needed`, the same effect hg-lnw.3 removed for the restore path. This is pre-existing at base, and spec 4.4 covers only the undo-restore case."},{"section":"Concerns","position":3,"key":"The hg-lnw.30 detection relies on the Restore intent still being present at the `Unknown` re-run. If `ClearOnReattach` lands between the lost response and the re-run, `seat_intent` is `None` and `launched:` is recorded, so the hg-lnw.3 break returns on that interleaving. The window is about one reconcile pass against an agent's check-in time, so it is unlikely. The test runs the clear only after the re-run.","text":"The hg-lnw.30 detection relies on the Restore intent still being present at the `Unknown` re-run. If `ClearOnReattach` lands between the lost response and the re-run, `seat_intent` is `None` and `launched:` is recorded, so the hg-lnw.3 break returns on that interleaving. The window is about one reconcile pass against an agent's check-in time, so it is unlikely. The test runs the clear only after the re-run."},{"section":"Concerns","position":4,"key":"`restart_covered` now accepts `launched:` alone, and `incarnation:changed_at` is never cleared. A keyed clone whose agent graph launched but which never reported a session can therefore get one `RelaunchOccupant` per incarnation whenever its fact reads `Unresolved` after an exit, long after the restart itself. The same already held for clones with sessions, so the rule is consistent. It is just broader than \"after a restart\".","text":"`restart_covered` now accepts `launched:` alone, and `incarnation:changed_at` is never cleared. A keyed clone whose agent graph launched but which never reported a session can therefore get one `RelaunchOccupant` per incarnation whenever its fact reads `Unresolved` after an exit, long after the restart itself. The same already held for clones with sessions, so the rule is consistent. It is just broader than \"after a restart\"."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":1,"key":"No test covers the \"resume fails\" half of spec section 4 row 4 (a keyless `--resume` relaunch whose agent exits at once); the code does not start a fresh agent there.","text":"No test covers the \"resume fails\" half of spec section 4 row 4 (a keyless `--resume` relaunch whose agent exits at once); the code does not start a fresh agent there."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":2,"key":"No test covers `ClearOnReattach` landing before the lost-response re-run of a keyless restore `StartAgent`.","text":"No test covers `ClearOnReattach` landing before the lost-response re-run of a keyless restore `StartAgent`."},{"section":"Untested scope","position":3,"key":"No test covers a keyless gate whose fact reads `Resolved` on a pane other than the clone's own, inside the grace;
